Preview of the Section 16 report:

Qualitative error analysis report

For the main target problem, Chimalapa Zoque, the direct zero-shot baseline already performs weakly (BLEU 14.46, chrF 37.64, Exact Match 0.00), and every multi-pass prompting strategy degrades that baseline. The least harmful strategy is recursive_bootstrapping_two_pass, but even that configuration falls to BLEU 8.70 and chrF 15.87. The goal of this section is therefore diagnostic: to identify which error categories actually dominate the failed improved runs, and to ensure that the narrative follows the computed evidence.

The denominator-normalized comparison is essential here. The baseline profile is computed over 12 unique target answers, whereas the improved aggregate profile is computed over 36 answer records because it pools the three tested strategies. The baseline target profile is mixed: structural_error 3/12 (0.25 per answer), morphological_error 3/12 (0.25 per answer), lexical_error 4/12 (0.33 per answer). By contrast, the aggregate improved-run profile is dominated by lexical_error 30/36 (0.83 per answer), hallucinated_rule 11/36 (0.31 per answer), structural_error 5/36 (0.14 per answer). This means the improved runs are not mainly characterized by morphology or semantic-role confusion under the current heuristics; instead, they are dominated by lexical failure, hallucinated-rule behaviour, and a smaller amount of structural error.

For the strongest strategy under the current ranking, recursive_bootstrapping_two_pass, the answer-level profile is: lexical_error 9/12 (0.75 per answer), hallucinated_rule 1/12 (0.08 per answer), structural_error 2/12 (0.17 per answer), morphological_error 0/12 (0.00 per answer), semantic_role_confusion 0/12 (0.00 per answer). This strategy still improves only 4 of 12 answers while worsening 8, so even the best multi-pass configuration remains clearly below the direct zero-shot baseline.

Structural error is therefore present, but it should be described as secondary rather than dominant. Under recursive_bootstrapping_two_pass, structure-related signals appear in 2/12 (0.17 per answer). This supports the claim that some answers recover part of the right material but assemble it in the wrong order or global configuration. The strongest structure-related instances under recursive_bootstrapping_two_pass appear in question(s) 4, 5.

Morphological error should not be presented as a dominant improved-run category. The improved aggregate profile contains 0/36 (0.00 per answer), and recursive_bootstrapping_two_pass contains 0/12 (0.00 per answer). The only place where morphology remains visible is the baseline target profile, where it appears in 3/12 (0.25 per answer). Accordingly, the final report should treat morphology as a baseline-side or weak residual signal here, not as one of the main improved-run dominant classes.

Lexical error is the clearest dominant class in the improved runs. Under recursive_bootstrapping_two_pass, lexical signals appear in 9/12 (0.75 per answer), and across all improved strategies they appear in 30/36 (0.83 per answer). This is why the section should not claim that the bottleneck is mainly non-lexical. In the present run, lexical failure is central, especially in question(s) 7, 9, 8.

Semantic-role confusion is not robustly supported as an independent dominant category in this run. The improved aggregate profile contains 0/36 (0.00 per answer), and recursive_bootstrapping_two_pass contains 0/12 (0.00 per answer). Accordingly, the final interpretation should not describe semantic-role confusion as recurring unless a future rerun actually raises these counts.

Hallucinated-rule behaviour is now supported not only by output degradation but also by direct inspection of the intermediate reasoning texts. For recursive_bootstrapping_two_pass, the non-final stages contain 3 rule-claim lines, 1 of which have no explicit support-token grounding, and 0 strong-claim lines. This does not prove hallucination in a strict causal sense, but it does provide memo-level evidence that unsupported generalizations are appearing before the final answers are produced.

Incomplete decomposition also remains strongly supported by the answer-level comparison. The best strategy, recursive_bootstrapping_two_pass, improves 4 answers but worsens 8, with mean sentence-level chrF delta -22.78. More elaborate strategies become progressively worse, which is consistent with the idea that extra reasoning passes amplify drift instead of stabilizing the solution.

At a broader level, this pattern also helps explain why Gemma behaves much better on easier ORDER cases than on a structurally richer hard case such as Chimalapa Zoque. In the baseline, the easier ORDER controls performed much better, especially Engenni 5 (BLEU 56.85, chrF 64.77) and Rapa Nui 5 (BLEU 32.58, chrF 57.22). The contamination check also remained stable for Engenni 5 (chrF retention 1.2590), Rapa Nui 5 (chrF retention 0.9588), which is more compatible with genuine structure-based reasoning than with simple dependence on original lexical surface forms. This is compatible with the idea that the model can exploit genuine structural regularities when the system is simpler, but struggles once many interacting regularities must be tracked simultaneously.

The safest final interpretation is therefore fully data-driven: in the current run, the dominant improved-run pattern is lexical failure, hallucinated-rule behaviour, and some structural error. Morphological instability and semantic-role confusion should not be presented as dominant improved-run categories here, because the calibrated counts do not support that claim. If a future rerun changes the counts, the narrative should change with them.
